The same exemption is written into this design: wave 5 safety rule (2) of 5.3 ("no output in the last 60 seconds and an empty `draft`; otherwise nothing is sent") gains "except the interrupt of `escalate --interrupt`, N7 of 5.9" (change 64).

FR-5, FR-6, FR-8, FR-9, FR-21, FR-22 and FR-26 to FR-28 are cited by this design and not changed: the orchestrator is the coordinator they describe. FR-10, FR-23 and FR-24 change by C21 only in who does the work, and their typed parts are built by the orchestrator pull request (DQ9).

### 6.4 The report scripts and an orchestrated item

An "orchestrated item" is an issue that has an entry file in `.dev/coordinator/orchestrated/` while this run's collection shows it open and assigned to the user (DQ12); entry files in `closed/` and `info/` make nothing orchestrated. The daily run never touches an orchestrated item (owner ruling 1, 2026-11-19); the living report is built from the entry files by `run.py refresh`, which also ends Step 12 of every daily run. The table lists every reader of `compare.json`, of the previous snapshot and of the entry file in the `agent-work-report` scripts (IronFlow copy, read for this design) and what each does for an orchestrated item once PR C is in.

| Reader | Reads | What it prints or does for an orchestrated item |
|---|---|---|
| `exclude.py` (new, PR C), run between Steps 4 and 5 | entry files in `orchestrated/`, `github-items.json`, `sessions.json`, `coverage.json` | removes the held issue, the pull request and the sessions its entry names, and lists their urls in `excluded.json`; an entry whose issue the run does not show open and assigned stays unexcluded; this is the only place that decides what is orchestrated. A ClickUp task linked to the issue is then matched to no GitHub item by `match.py` and becomes an item of its own |
| `compare.py` `main` (73-122) | `excluded.json`, previous `snapshot.json` | drops the excluded urls from the previous snapshot, so the item is not new, changed, unchanged, carried or closed |
| `common.py` `to_write` (211-217) | `classified.json`, `compare.json` | never sees the item, so never queues it for the model |
| `render.py` `main` and `entry` in a daily run | `classified.json` | never sees the item: no block, no placeholder, no update marker |
| `check.py` `main` (104-133) | `overview.md`, previous snapshot | never sees the item in the entries; the carry-forward check (127-133) skips the urls of `excluded.json`, so the item is not reported as lost |
| `page.py` `change_of` (107-112) and `main` (149-201) in a daily run | `compare.json`, `overview.md` | never see the item; `main` still copies its page to the shared path (198-199), and `refresh --run` overwrites that copy in the same step |
| `run.py` (`passed`, `gate`, `close_run`) | `script-checks.md`, `<run>/report.html` | reads no `compare.json`; unaffected, because `refresh --run` never writes `<run>/report.html` |
| `run.py refresh` (new, PR C), at the end of Step 12 as `refresh --run <run>` and at each milestone | entry files, the run's or the last completed run's `overview.md` | reconciles each entry with GitHub (a closed or unassigned issue moves to `closed/` with GitHub's real reason), prunes `closed/` and `info/` after `info_ttl_days`, keeps the non-orchestrated blocks as they are, adds one block per entry file and builds only the shared `.dev/coordinator/report.html`, never `<run>/report.html`; no model call; without `--run` it does nothing while a daily run holds the lock |
| `render.py` `entry` (126-174), called by `refresh` for an entry file | entry file | every written part, `happened` (lines 136-140) and each session's `<status>: <reason>` (lines 142-150) come from the file; no update marker, no open slot; it never prints "Nothing since the previous report" and never calls `carried_status` for such an item |
| `page.py` `card` (115-146) in `refresh` | the block `entry` made, the entry file's fixed fields | the card shows the same text as the block, with the orchestrator and worker links (5.7) |
| `common.py` `previous_notes` (205-208) | `notes.json` | unchanged: it holds only the items of the daily run |


## 7. Carried over and dropped

### 7.1 Carried over from #450 and the design (every item of the history comment)

| # | Carried-over item | Disposition | Where it lands |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Reuse of the built `issue-watch` code as the poll step: `runner.py`, `record.py`, `audit.py`, `launch.py`, call-budget, error classification and rate-limit code of `github.py`; `cycle.py` reshaped | Reused. `runner.py` and the `github.py` budget, classification and rate-limit code are kept as they are; `record.py`, `audit.py` and `launch.py` are reshaped (schema 2, 62 reasons, expected-repo origin check, wake line, session start); `cycle.py` is reshaped (4.5) | PR A; per symbol in 4.10 |
| 2a | Safety feature: at most one launch per issue, a durable dispatch record written before the launch | Kept in a stated new form: the poll writes the durable `pending` entry before any wake and allows at most 3 wake attempts per issue; "at most one launch" becomes "at most one claim and one worker start per issue", enforced by the orchestrator (DQ3, 5.5) because the poll no longer launches the work. This is a change in what the feature guarantees | PR A (record), PR B (state); open decision OD9 |
| 2b | A lock against two runs at once | Kept unchanged: `flock` on `state_dir/lock`, a second cycle exits 75 (`record.py:282`, `cycle.py:698-699`); the lock test (setup runbook 8.2) stays | PR A |
| 2c | A GitHub call budget | Kept: `CALL_CAP` 35, reserved calls resized (4.6) | PR A |
| 2d | An audit log | Kept: `audit.jsonl`, 5 MiB rotation, a closed reason table (62 reasons, one test pins it) | PR A |
| 2e | A dry-run mode | Kept and extended: writes no wake file, mailbox message, typed line or session (4.9) | PR A |
| 2f | Only a validated integer reaches the poll script's launch prompt (not the orchestrator's handoff prompt) | Kept in its new form: only a string that matches the wake id pattern reaches the typed line, built by one function that refuses anything else; the issue number is no longer in the line at all (3.2). The property test that only a validated value reaches the prompt is kept and rewritten for `build_wake_line`. The handoff prompt is outside the rule; it quotes issue text as data | PR A |
| 2g | An origin check | Kept per repo (4.4); `check_origin` takes the expected repo | PR A |
| 3 | The live test against real `gh` and Orca on throwaway assigned issues, replacing every guessed `_assumed_shape` fixture, and a written live-test record | Kept. The 19 fixtures in `tests/issue_watch/fixtures/_assumed_shape/` are handled: `gh_api_user_include.json`, `gh_api_user_include_429.json` and the five `gh_stderr_*.json` are re-captured; `gh_issue_list.json` and `gh_issue_reread.json` are replaced by captured search and re-read replies; `orca_launch_*.json` (4) are replaced by captured `terminal create` and `terminal send` receipts; `gh_graphql_*.json`, `graphql_edits.json`, `graphql_project_items.json`, `rest_comments_marker.json`, `rest_events_page.json` are deleted with the code they pin. New captures: the search replies for S1 to S4, `orchestration send --to run:<id>` from a plain shell, `orca automations create` with a `*/5` trigger. The UNVERIFIED rows of `issue-watcher.md` section 15 are carried into section 12 | PR E (the live test is owner-run) |
| 4 | The 24-hour shadow run, the two-process lock test and the restart test (`issue-watcher-setup.md` sections 7, 8.2, 8.3) before switching on | Kept; the shadow run is the scheduled automation with `--dry-run` in its command (4.8); the pass condition becomes "every cycle ends 0 or an understood reason, `would_wake` appears for the assigned throwaway issue and nothing else happens" | build procedure steps 9 and 10 |
| 5a | FU-3, narrower GitHub token | Stays open. The `read:project` scope is no longer needed; the token needs search and issue write (for the poll as well as for the orchestrator: the hold label), comment and merge rights on listed repos (UNVERIFIED which fine-grained scopes search needs, U11) | after the live test |
| 5b | FU-4, alert after three bad cycles | Stays open; the sink now exists: the Orca unread mark of DQ2 | follow-up |
| 5c | FU-5, cheaper GitHub reads (for example conditional requests) | Stays open; trigger is call-budget pressure (UNVERIFIED whether the search API accepts conditional requests, U12) | follow-up |
| 5d | FU-6, permissions of launched sessions | Stays open and moves to the orchestrated worker: its permission mode and the guard-less condition of DQ7; settled before the first real issue | PR B plan, owner decision OD1 |
| 5e | FU-8, automatic launch retry | Done by design: it is the orchestrator's own single retry in wave 4 (ruling R5); `retry_argv` is removed | PR A, PR B |
| 5f | FU-10, service-level review after 7 days live | Kept; `slo_numbers` measures assignment to acknowledgment; scheduled at switch-on | step 10 |
| 5g | FU-11, size bound for the dispatch store | Stays open; the key is now `owner/repo#N`; trigger is a store above 1 MiB | follow-up |
| 5h | FU-12, check that the state folder is on a local disk | Kept: the network file system deny-list stays in `load_config` (`cycle.py:280-283`); the allow-list stays a follow-up | PR A |
| 6 | #442's launch checks (OB-H1, OB-H2, OB-H3, OB-CTX) before any agent launch | Not a gate for this design's workers (DQ7): they are never governed. The checks stay required for any governed launch, and for the orchestrator if the owner chooses to bind its workers (OD1, option B). The live test records `ls ~/.claude/state/launch-level/sessions/` before and after the first worker starts, to prove it is unbound (the check of `issue-pipeline-fu1.md`, section f) | owner decision OD1; PR E |
| 7 | Owner questions Q1 to Q4 of the design | Q1 (one launch per run) is replaced by the new-since-last-poll rule (4.3). Q2 is answered by the orchestrator's own check and single retry, confirmed by Clay (wave 4). Q3 is answered by exponential backoff (4.6; the flat 3600 s clamp goes). Q4 is accepted as written: an unrecognized `gh` error is treated as `gh_auth_failed` on shared reads until the live test (`github.py:269-275`) | PR A, PR B |

### 7.2 Dropped on purpose, with the file-by-file removal plan

Removed in PR D (retire the gate) and PR A (poll core). "Else used by" is what a search found; PR A and PR D re-run that search before deleting and keep what a user needs. Counts are occurrences of the name in `tests` and `src` Python files (grep, 2026-11-19).

An ordering fact decides the PR order (section 8): `issue_pipeline` imports from `issue_watch`, so PR A cannot remove those symbols while `issue_pipeline` exists, and `main.py` imports `issue_pipeline` at start-up, so a broken import breaks the whole `superclaude` command. The imports: `record.FileStore`, `LockError`, `LockHeld` (`approval_record.py:32`); `predicates.valid_issue_number` (`brief.py:16`); `predicates.EditEvidence`, `LabelEvent` (`reads.py:35`) and `CommentEdit` (`stage_predicates.py:42`); `github.CallBudget`, `Read`, `_argv_rest_page`, `_check`, `_pos_int`, `_rest_read`, `_text`, `_time` (`reads.py:25-34`); `cycle.load_config`, `cycle.validate_approvers` and `config.approval_label`, `config.approvers_file` (`cli.py:227-228`), `cycle.ConfigError` (`cli.py:379`); `github.user`, `github.reread` (`cli.py:276, 279`); `runner.Runner`, `SubprocessRunner` (`cli.py:18`).

| Dropped item | Files and symbols removed | Tests removed or rewritten | Else used by |

### 7.3 What guard G2 still needs after PR D

`launch-level-guard.sh` has two G2 rules (`issue-pipeline-guard-g2.md`):

| G2 rule | Lines | After PR D | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. TAMPER class for the coordinator-side `issue-pipeline` commands that write approval rows and the worker's release file | 441-444 | Removed (guard and plugin copy, byte-identical) | The verbs it names no longer exist; the guard's own approval path for a governed worker (`sessions/<sid>.ok`, written by a person, FR-L-07, `launch-level-guard.sh:270`, `:347-351`) is a different mechanism and stays |
| 2. A bound worker may run `orchestration send` and `ask` only as one plain call with no `--to`, so the message goes to its own coordinator Run | 489, 497-520 | Kept | It does not depend on the approval flow; it is needed by any governed worker that reports to a coordinator, and by every worker if the owner binds orchestrated workers (OD1, option B); it has its own tests, which stay |

Because the guard is a security control, the design note requires an independent review of any change to it (`issue-pipeline-guard-g2.md`, header); PR D therefore carries that review as an acceptance check, and the comments that cite `issue-pipeline-guard-g2.md` (lines 441 and 500) are updated to say the rule 1 text is retired. Other files that name the dropped items and are not code: `docs/architecture/SUPERVISED-ORCHESTRATION-TECHNICAL-REFERENCE.md`, `docs/designs/orchestrator-mode.md`, `docs/memory/reflect/deviation-patterns-issue-331-github-issue-watcher.md` and the fixture `tests/review/fixtures/reflect_cards/yaml-deviation-class.yaml` are checked by PR D (grep) and edited only where they state a rule that is gone. The design docs `issue-watcher.md`, `issue-watcher-setup.md`, `issue-pipeline-fu1.md`, `issue-pipeline-guard-g2.md`, `issue-pipeline-guard-g2-no-to.md` and `sc-issue-claim-pipeline-flag.md` keep their history and get a status banner at the top that points to this design (issue 655, "Documentation Needs").


## 8. The pull request split

### 8.1 Pieces, files, acceptance

For this build no pull request is merged by the orchestrator or by any worker: each stops when CI is green, Augment and Codex have finished on the final head and no review thread is open, and the owner does the QA and the merge. The merge behavior of the runtime orchestrator (wave 7) is unchanged.

Every PR: base `master` (as issue 655 says; `origin/integration` already holds PR #650, so a PR that edits `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` rebases onto it, accuracy-check.md); one feature branch from `origin/master`; opened only through `/sc:pr-submit --monitor 3 --head <branch>`; the regression gate passes on its own (`make test`, `make lint`, `uv run ruff format --check src/ tests/`, `make verify-deps`, `make sync-dev` then `make verify-sync`); `enabled: false` stays; a PR that touches a gate or validator file carries the "## Generator-Constraint Considered" section. No `.claude/` path is ever staged (CLAUDE.md).

### 8.2 Order and what runs in parallel (confirms issue 655 section 13, with two changes)

Change 1: PR D moves before PR A and PR B. Issue 655 puts D after B "so the old code stays until the new flow works". Nothing runs the old code (the watcher has `enabled: false` and has never run live), and the order cannot hold: PR A cannot remove the symbols `issue_pipeline` imports (7.2), and the old flow's plan-approval rules would contradict the new section of `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` that PR B adds. Retiring first removes the shim PR A would otherwise need. This is decision OD10, decided by owner ruling 9 (2026-11-19): retire first. (The other order would have kept the imported symbols `LabelEvent`, `CommentEdit`, `EditEvidence`, the `github` readers, `validate_approvers` and the approval fields of `Config` in PR A until PR D deleted them with the package; it is not taken.)
Change 2: PR B's new `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` section is part of PR B, not of D, because the first worker needs it (ADD1). PR B opens after PR A merges, because its `superclaude orchestrator repos` verbs import `issue_watch/repos.py`; B can be built against section 4.4 before that.

| Piece | Starts | Waits for |
|---|---|---|
| This design and its review | now | nothing; the owner's approval gates everything below |
| #651 (issue templates) | now | nothing (another worker) |
| O1 create the orchestrator repo, O2 move the spec and state | after approval, in parallel with everything | nothing to start; B's local end-to-end tests and C need O1 |
| PR D | after approval | nothing |
| PR A | after approval (can be written in parallel with D; merges after D) | D merged |
| PR P: the `/sc:issue` plan mode | after approval (can be written in parallel with A) | D merged |
| PR B | after approval (written in parallel with A) | A merged and PR P merged before its PR is opened (wave 0 calls the mode; PR B's tests use a fake of the call); T1 to T13 before its PR is opened; O1; #651 for wave 0 at run time only |
| Issue 668 (the closeout skill): a dependency, not a 655 piece | after approval (its own spec and approval) | its pull request must be merged before the live test (PR E); it is built in parallel with PR A and PR B; PR B's wave 8 does not wait for it (its tests use a fake closeout, 5.10 point (e)) |
| PR C | after approval | O1 |
| Live test (owner-run), then PR E | after A, B, P and O1 | A, B, P, O1 |
| Shadow run (24 hours, dry-run automation), then lock test, restart test | after the live test and PR E | the live test, PR E |
| Switch-on (O3, O4, `enabled: true`, first real cycles) and O5 | last | the shadow run, PR C, PR D, #651 |


### 8.3 PR P: the `/sc:issue` plan mode (ruling 31)

Clay's ruling 31: all plan creation flows through `/sc:issue`, so a mode is added that fills the three sections of an EXISTING issue (for example `/sc:issue --plan <N>`), reusing create's drafting workers and the #651 templates, as its own small pull request in the 655 split, built before PR B. What follows is the smallest mechanism. Every statement about a current file was read on `origin/master` on 2026-11-19 and the line numbers are that file's. It is the design for the pull request's author, not the pull request.

**What is missing today.** `/sc:issue` has two modes. The argument hint (`commands/issue.md:9`) and the mode list (`sc-issue-protocol/SKILL.md:15-18`) name only `--claim` and `--create`. `--claim` assigns the issue, checks it and posts two comments (`SKILL.md:17`); `--create` files a new issue from one of four templates (`SKILL.md:18`, `refs/create.md` Waves 0 to 5), and its Wave 4 fills `## Priority`, `## Fix approach` and `## Test plan` for that new issue (`refs/create.md:57-63`, the Priority rule set at line 71). No mode writes those sections into an issue that already exists.

**Files that change** (all under `src/superclaude/`; the `.claude/` copies come from `make sync-dev` and are never staged, CLAUDE.md; master has no `plugins/` copy of the issue command or skill):
1. `commands/issue.md`: the argument hint (line 9) and the two-mode summary at the top gain `--plan <number|url> [--depth quick|standard|deep]`; the Options table (from line 34) gains a row for `--plan` and one for `--remaining <file>` (ruling 36); the STOP rules (lines 50 to 59) gain the rules below; the Examples (line 61) gain `/sc:issue --plan 302` and `/sc:issue --plan 302 --depth standard`; Outputs (line 74) gains a plan row; Boundaries, `Will` (line 89) and `Will Not` (line 96), gain the plan lines.
2. `skills/sc-issue-protocol/SKILL.md`: the description (line 3) and the mode list (lines 15 to 18) gain the third mode; the Files table (lines 24 to 36) gains `refs/plan.md`; the GitHub write rule (line 74) gains the one new write; a "Plan mode" section follows "Create mode" (lines 94 to 103) and says "Load `refs/plan.md` now"; the return contract (from line 105) gains `mode: plan`; the error table (from line 152) gains the plan rows; `Will` (line 179) and `Will Not` (line 187) gain the plan lines.
3. New `skills/sc-issue-protocol/refs/plan.md`: the waves of plan mode (below).
4. `tests/skills/test_issue_protocol.py` and `tests/commands/test_issue_command.py` (both exist on master): the new cases of the row "PR P" of 9.1.
No new template. The heading block is the one that already ends every issue template (`## Priority`, `## Fix approach`, `## Test plan` at lines 35, 39 and 50 of `templates/issue-feature-simple.md`, 43, 47 and 58 of `issue-bug-simple.md`, 69, 73 and 84 of `issue-feature-complex.md`, 77, 81 and 92 of `issue-bug-complex.md`), and `refs/plan.md` points to `refs/create.md:59-81` for the rule so that the rule stays written once.

**The flag and its STOP rules.** `--plan <number|url>` takes an issue number (`302`, `#302`) or an issue URL on the origin repo, as `--claim` does, and an optional `--depth quick|standard|deep` (default `quick`, as in create mode). The existing wording of lines 50 to 59 is extended, not replaced:
- Exactly one of `--claim`, `--create` and `--plan`: none or more than one is STOP with the Usage block (today "Both or neither", line 51).
- `--mode` or `--type` with `--plan`: STOP `E-CREATE-ONLY-FLAG`, whose message becomes "--mode and --type apply to --create only; --depth applies to --create and --plan" (the issue's own text decides what is missing). `--depth` with `--claim` stays refused (line 53).
- `--pipeline` or `--submit` with `--plan`: STOP `E-CLAIM-ONLY-FLAG` (message unchanged: "--pipeline and --submit apply to --claim only"; line 57 today covers `--create`).
- An empty `--plan` argument: STOP with the Usage block.
- After the issue is read in Wave 0: not found on the origin repo: STOP `E-ISSUE-NOT-FOUND`; the number is a pull request: STOP `E-PLAN-NOT-AN-ISSUE` (U55); the issue is closed: STOP `E-PLAN-ISSUE-CLOSED`; one of the three headings occurs twice (not fillable): STOP `E-PLAN-REPEATED-HEADING`, nothing is written; all three sections already pass the check (each heading exactly once, non-empty, not only "Not known yet": the rule of `plan_check`, 4.11 point 1): nothing is written and the result is `already_planned`, which is a success.

**Waves of `refs/plan.md`** (numbered as create mode's, so a reader of `refs/create.md` can follow):
- Wave 0, prerequisites (the calling session): as `refs/create.md` Wave 0 (`origin_repo.py` prints `<R>`; `gh auth status`); `<D>` is `.dev/issues/<N>/`; read the issue live (`gh issue view <N> --repo <R> --json number,title,body,state,labels,comments,updatedAt`); apply the STOP rules above; write `<D>/plan-input.md`.
- Waves 1 to 3 are not run: create's lookup lists labels, issue types and the Complexity field, and its flag and template steps choose what plan mode does not need.
- Wave 4, draft: the worker of the depth, as in `refs/create.md:49-56` (`issue-worker-t3` with `/sc:analyze --depth quick`, `issue-worker-t2` with `/sc:analyze`, `issue-worker-t1` with `/sc:analyze --depth deep`, the T1 to T2 fallback of `refs/create.md:34` and `refs/tiers.md`) reads `plan-input.md` and writes `<D>/plan-sections.md` holding the text for each of the three sections that is absent, empty or only "Not known yet" (the heading and its text for an absent one, the text alone for an empty or placeholder one), using the rule of `refs/create.md:59-81` ("Not known yet" is not allowed in these three sections; a gap is stated as an assumption in the section and listed in `<D>/draft-notes.md`). Plan mode asks no question: the contradiction prompt of `refs/create.md:82` is not used, because the orchestrator calls this mode with no person present.
- Wave 5, write (the calling session, directly): re-read the body (`gh issue view <N> --repo <R> --json body,state`); if it changed since Wave 0, merge once more, and a second change is STOP `E-PLAN-RACE` with nothing written. The new body is built from the re-read body by exactly three kinds of change: an **absent** heading is appended with its text at the end; a heading present **once** with an empty section or only "Not known yet" (or only an HTML comment) is **filled in place**: the heading line stays, and the text between it and the next `## ` heading (or the end) is replaced by the drafted text; a heading that occurs **twice** is not touched and is STOP `E-PLAN-REPEATED-HEADING` before anything is written. The marker `<!-- sc:issue:plan -->` is added once, on its own line directly under the first heading filled or appended. Every byte outside the sections filled or appended is unchanged. Then `gh issue edit <N> --repo <R> --body-file <D>/plan-body.md`; read the body back and check that every byte outside the filled sections is unchanged, that each of the three headings occurs exactly once and that `plan_check` now passes (else STOP `E-PLAN-WRITE-FAILED`). This one body edit is the only GitHub write of plan mode: no comment, no label, no assignment, no ClickUp call, no worktree and no launch.
- Return contract, printed last with `mode: plan`: `status` (`success`, `already_planned` or `stopped`), `issue_url`, `sections_added` (closed tokens `priority`, `fix_approach`, `test_plan`), `sections_replaced` (closed tokens `fix_approach`, `test_plan`; only with `--remaining`), `assumptions` (a count), `depth`.

**Re-plan with `--remaining` (ruling 36, AD-D7).** A left-open issue HAS a plan, so plain plan mode stops with `already_planned`. The smallest extension is one more option, `--plan <number|url> --remaining <file> [--depth ...]`, which updates the plan of a claimed issue to the scope that is left. It is not a new mode and adds no file to PR P: the same four files change (the command file, `SKILL.md`, `refs/plan.md` with one more section "Re-plan", and the two test files), and the section of `refs/plan.md` is about 20 lines. It is small enough for PR P; no separate pull request is needed.
- The file: UTF-8 text; the first line is `round: <n>` (n is 2 or more); then one line `- <criterion>` for each unmet criterion, at least 1 and at most 20 lines of at most 300 characters. The orchestrator's verb `handback` writes it (wave 8, 5.3); a person can write one by hand.
- STOP rules added: `--remaining` without `--plan`, or with `--claim` or `--create`: STOP `E-PLAN-ONLY-FLAG` ("--remaining applies to --plan only"); a file that is missing, empty, over the limits or without the `round:` line: STOP `E-REMAINING-FILE`; an issue whose body fails `plan_check` (a plan is missing): STOP `E-REMAINING-NEEDS-PLAN` (plain plan mode comes first); a repeated heading: STOP `E-PLAN-REPEATED-HEADING` as in plain mode; a body that already holds the marker `<!-- sc:issue:replan round=<n> -->` for this round: nothing is written and the result is `already_planned` (a rerun after a kill writes nothing twice).
- Wave 0 as plain mode (the issue is read live, the STOP rules apply, `<D>/plan-input.md` is written and now also holds the remaining file and the comments of the issue). Waves 1 to 3 are not run. Wave 4, draft: the worker of the depth, with the same T1 to T2 fallback, writes `<D>/plan-sections.md` with the replacement text for `## Fix approach` and `## Test plan` only (`## Priority` is never touched). Each replaced section opens with the list "Done so far (up to round <n-1>):" in which every criterion of the old section that is not in the remaining list is kept as a done item (`- [x] <criterion>`), and then "Remaining (round <n>):" with the unmet criteria: in `## Fix approach` how each is done, in `## Test plan` how each is tested (the tests of the done items stay listed as already passing). "Not known yet" is not allowed (the rule of `refs/create.md:59-81`). No question is asked.
- Wave 5, write (the calling session): re-read the body; a change since wave 0 is merged once, and a second change is STOP `E-PLAN-RACE`. The new body is the re-read body with exactly two sections replaced in place (the heading stays, the text between it and the next `## ` heading is replaced; a line that is a hidden marker of the skill, `<!-- sc:issue:... -->`, inside a replaced section is kept, directly under its heading, so the plan marker and the create marker are not lost), plus the marker `<!-- sc:issue:replan round=<n> -->` once, on its own line under `## Fix approach`. Every byte outside the two sections is unchanged. The old text of the two sections is saved as `<D>/plan-before-round-<n>.md` (local) and stays in the issue's edit history. Then `gh issue edit <N> --repo <R> --body-file <D>/plan-body.md`; read back; the checks of plain mode apply (outside bytes unchanged, each heading once, `plan_check` passes, else `E-PLAN-WRITE-FAILED`). The one body edit is the only GitHub write: no comment, no label, no assignment.
- UNVERIFIED (U60): that this option works when called from inside wave 2 (the same question as U55 (1)), and that a worker of the depth keeps the done items faithfully (a quality of the drafting that no script checks; the hand-back test uses a fake).

**How the orchestrator uses it.** The plan call of 4.11 point 5: wave 0 runs `Skill sc:issue-protocol` with `--plan <N> --depth <d>` in the repo's checkout, `<d>` coming from `plan_tier` by the table of `refs/intake.md`. For a hand-back round wave 2 runs `/sc:issue --plan <N> --remaining <file> --depth <d>` (ruling 36; the re-plan above). A person can run `/sc:issue --plan <N>` by hand for any issue. Nothing is added to `grants`: the body write is the grant `issue.body.append_plan_sections` (DQ6; its name is kept and it also covers the in-place fill of an empty section, see A-6), and the skill's rule that only the calling session writes to GitHub (`SKILL.md:74`) stays true.

**Order.** PR P comes after PR D and before PR B (ruling 31: "built before PR B"); PR B opens after PR P merges. UNVERIFIED (U55): that a Skill call of this mode from inside wave 0 works, the depth table, and the pull-request check.

## 9. Test strategy

### 9.1 Automated tests per piece

All use fakes for `gh`, `orca`, `git` and the clock (the `Runner` seam of `tests/issue_watch/`), never the real orgs. Existing tests that stay: module boundaries, no-shell, no-text, security invariants, the lock and crash-injection tests.

A label that a hand took off is confirmed by one live read, becomes `hold_removed_by_hand`, and is never put back in any later cycle (a test runs five cycles); a label put back by hand restarts `first_held_at`; an issue that leaves S1 and returns is held afresh. The poll never puts a failing issue in `assigned[]`, with or without a label. A plan request is written once per hold: `plan_requested_at` is set when the item is written, is never cleared and never set again (a restart of the poll and a second poll process on the same store send nothing more), whether or not the item was acknowledged; it is re-delivered under rule 4 of 4.3 only while it has not been acknowledged. A request that is `taken` and whose plan record (named by its `plan_requested_at`) is `in_progress` or missing 60 minutes after `plan_acked_at` raises `plan_stuck` once, is set `stuck` and nothing is sent again; a record of that request that is `done` or `failed` ends the request; the count of the place ends at the first of the record final, `plan_stuck` raised, or the hold ended (P5A-1); a final record of an earlier request of the same key is ignored, and a second request for the key after a final record is counted and read through its own record. Baseline: an issue in the baseline is never labeled. A held issue has no entry in `entries` and is woken once, in the first cycle after the hold is lifted. `--dry-run` writes no label and no comment and lists `would_hold`, `would_release` and `would_request`. The wake file: `plan_requests[]` items match the patterns of 3.7 point 2, `assigned[]` has no `hold` or `missing` field, and the golden file shared with PR B is updated. |
