### 9.2 The local end-to-end tests of issue 655 section 14: how each is run (T1 to T9), and the ones this design adds (T10 to T13)

The author runs them before PR B is opened, in a test orchestrator repo with its own `repos.yaml` (the production list is untouched), and puts the results in the PR description. Rule for all thirteen: nothing below is created until the author has asked the owner by `ask` (or in the owner's session) and named each repo, issue, pull request and automation to be created; throwaway repos are private, named `orchestrator-e2e-<n>`, and deleted by the owner (deleting a repo is a human-only action, rule 1). The poll runs first as `once --dry-run` and only then for real, with `enabled: true` in a test poll configuration, on a test Orca workspace.

| # | Test | What it needs | How it is run | Pass |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | Throwaway issues assigned to the user in two repos, one per org | 2 private throwaway repos (one in IronbellyOrg, one in IronbellyGroup) each with a local checkout; 1 issue in each, assigned to the user | list both repos in the test `repos.yaml`; run a real cycle; let the session take both | one wake lists both issues; the origin check ran per repo; each worker's worktree is in its own repo (`orca worktree ps`) |
| T2 | Which user it polls for | 2 issues in one throwaway repo: one assigned to the user's login, one to another user; plus one co-assigned (user and the other user) | needs one other user who can be assigned (U15) | the first is taken; the second and the co-assigned one are skipped `assigned_other`; nothing else is claimed |
| T3 | A repo with no local checkout | 1 listed throwaway repo with `checkout: null` and a `clone_parent`; 1 unlisted repo with an assigned issue; 1 repo whose checkout `origin` is a different repo | one issue in each; run the poll and the session | the first is cloned by `setup-clone` and started in its own worktree; the unlisted one is skipped `repo_not_listed` and no worker exists; the wrong-origin one is `origin_mismatch` and no worker exists |
| T4 | Two orchestrators, or two users, see the same issue | 1 issue; two orchestrator sessions as the same login on two Runs (two test orchestrator repos on the host, or two hosts if available); a second account as a second user | assign the issue; run one poll against each test orchestrator repo in the same second, so each poll routes the issue to its own orchestrator (one poll never routes it twice, 5.7) | exactly one claim comment with the marker, one lease winner (the lowest comment id), one `worker-start`; the loser's lease is edited to `released`; the second user's orchestrator skips an issue assigned to the first |
| T5 | Restart in the middle of each wave | 1 issue per wave (or one issue walked through the waves) | a failpoint, active only with `SUPERCLAUDE_TEST_FAILPOINTS=1`, stops `superclaude orchestrator state` right after an `intent` line; then the session is killed and `/sc:orchestrator --resume` runs | no second lease, claim comment, Task or `worker-start` after each of the ten waves; `run-use` resumed the same Run |
| T6 | A worker that dies, stalls, or asks | 3 issues whose worker brief is a scripted stand-in: exits at once; stays silent; sends one `ask` | wave 5 for each | `worker-list` states map to the FR-9 states; the stalled one gets a mailbox check-in and, unfixed the same day, rule 7; the asking one gets `reply`; the dead start is rule 8 after one retry |
| T7 | An issue with no plan | 1 issue with no Priority, Fix approach or Test plan; 1 issue so empty the agent cannot fill a section | wave 0 (the plan call) | the first gets all three sections appended with the plan marker; the second escalates under rule 5 and the body is unchanged |
| T8 | PR watch and merge on a real throwaway PR | 1 throwaway repo with a CI workflow that can be made to fail, a review agent installed (or the `--reviews` snapshot faked for the merge-gate cases), and 6 pull requests: failing check, unfinished review, open thread, no `worker_done`, head moved, clean | waves 6 and 7 on each | each of the first five is held with its reason and nothing is merged; the clean one is merged with the repo's method and its head sha, and the issue is closed; no `--auto`, `--admin` or `--delete-branch` in any call (read from the audit of `gh` argv) |
| T9 | The report entry from claim to close | the issues of T1 and T8 | read `.dev/coordinator/orchestrated/` and `report.html` at each milestone | added at claim; updated at the milestones only (not on every message, not hourly for a green pull request waiting); shows the human assignee, the orchestrator's link and the worker's `orca://session/` link; moved to `closed/` at close and gone from the open report |
| T10 (added by this design, 5.7) | Four issues of one repo | 1 throwaway repo with `max_in_flight` 3 and 4 issues assigned to the user; scripted stand-in workers | assign the four issues together; run the poll; then kill the session of orchestrator 1 | 3 issues go to `orchestrator-<short>-1` and the 4th starts `orchestrator-<short>-2`; no issue has two claim comments; after the kill the poll restarts orchestrator 1 with the same tag and Run and its three issues resume at their recorded steps; 30 minutes after the last issue is done both orchestrators have ended and no session is left |
| T11 (added by this design, 4.11) | The hold lifecycle | 1 throwaway repo, listed in the test `repos.yaml`; 10 issues assigned to the user: (a) all three sections filled in; (b) the three headings with one empty; (c) no sections at all, with a fake clock; (d) like (b) with `hold:backlog` placed by hand; (e) like (b), then `hold:plan` taken off by hand; (f) like (a), then a section is emptied after the wake and before the claim; (g) like (a), then the body is edited by a second account after the claim; (h) like (b), and nobody fills the empty section; (i) like (b) with `## Test plan` written twice; (j) like (c), in a repo whose test orchestrator already holds three issues in flight | needs one other account (U15); `/sc:issue --plan` is a fake until PR P is merged, and the real mode runs in the live test after it; run `once --dry-run` first, then for real; the fake clock moves the hour | (a) is woken at once, no label, no comment; (b) gets the label and no comment and no wake, and no plan request in the first hour; when the empty section is filled in the label is removed at the next cycle (`hold_released`) and the issue is woken in the cycle after; (c) gets the label, and a change at minute 50 (a comment) moves the plan request to minute 110; with no change a `plan_requests[]` item is written once at minute 60, the orchestrator writes the plan record `plan-<key>.<requested_at>.json` (`plan_call_started`) first and then acknowledges `taken`, the fake call is made to last 25 minutes and the poll neither re-delivers the item at minute 10 or 20 nor marks it abandoned; the call appends the three headings (the issue had none), `plan-check` passes, the record is `done`, the orchestrator writes no label and no lease and claims nothing, the poll lifts the hold at its next cycle and sends a normal assignment in the one after; three kill windows (P5A-1): a kill after the record is written and before the acknowledgment leaves a record `in_progress` and an unacknowledged wake, the poll re-delivers the item after 10 minutes, wave 0 finds the record of that request, makes no second record, acknowledges `taken` and the call runs once, nothing written twice; a kill after the acknowledgment and before the end of the call, then a restart, resumes the record and the poll neither re-delivers nor marks `abandoned`; a request for the same key made after the first record is `done` (the hold is taken off by hand and put back, and the hour passes again) has a new `requested_at`, makes a second file and gets its own call, the old file stays on disk and the poll reads only the new one, so the poll counts the new request as a place and does not read it as finished; a call made to hang past 60 minutes after the acknowledgment gives `plan_stuck` once (exit 7, unread mark, `plan_request: stuck`, the place no longer counted) and no second request, and so does an acknowledged request whose record file is removed by the test (no record, 60 minutes after `plan_acked_at`); a linked branch or pull request made at minute 30 keeps the request back until an hour after its time; (d) nothing at all is written for three cycles and an hour: no wake, no label write, no comment, no plan request, no store entry; when a person removes the label the issue is treated as new; (e) the label is not put back, the issue is not woken (`hold_removed_by_hand`), and unassigning and assigning it again holds it afresh; no `hold:plan` is ever written on an issue that was in the baseline; (f) the live re-check at the start of wave 1 catches the edit before the lease: no lease comment, no claim comment, status `skipped`, reason `plan_missing`; (g) nothing is re-held and the worker is not stopped; the report entry gets one line naming the second account's person and no other action is taken; an edit made by the user's own login adds no line; (h) the plan call fills the empty section in place: its heading occurs once, every byte outside that section is unchanged, the marker appears once, `plan-check` passes and the poll lifts the hold; (i) the call stops with `E-PLAN-REPEATED-HEADING` and writes nothing, the record is `failed`, a row 9 escalation names `repeated_heading` and no second request is sent; (j) at minute 60 no `plan_requests[]` item is written and `plan_requested_at` is not set, no new orchestrator is started, the next cycles try again, and the request is sent in the first cycle after one of the three issues has ended |
| T12 (added by this design, 5.10) | Wave 8's call of the closeout after five kinds of merge (the issue done, merged by a person, squash-merged, merged but not done, merged and unclear), the hand-back of a not-done issue for another round and its bound (ruling 36), and what waves 5 and 6 do with a pull request closed unmerged | 1 throwaway repo with one issue for each case (a) to (m) and the pull requests each needs, each with a scripted stand-in worker: (a) merged by the orchestrator; (b) merged by a person while its worker is still running (the PR #661 case); (c) squash-merged with the branch deleted; (d) closed without merging; (e) merged but delivering only part of a multi-part issue, a two-round issue (the fake closeout answers "not done" with two unmet criteria after round 1 and "done" after round 2, ruling 36); (f) merged with the fake closeout answering "unclear"; (g) no pull request: one state file seeded as `escalated` with a last step that is not a closeout step (an issue that waits for Clay's OK before a merge, escalated under the table of 5.6), to test the rule of 5.7 at its edge; (h) the cap: the fake closeout answers "not done" after each of rounds 1 to 4, with an unmet list that shrinks by one line each round; (i) no progress: the fake closeout answers "not done" with the same unmet lines after rounds 1 and 2 (in another case and order the second time); (j) the failures of a hand-back: the fake `/sc:issue --plan --remaining` made to fail twice; a worktree in round 2 that holds only the copied claim folder `.dev/issues/<N>/` (untracked, usable) and another with an edited tracked file (not usable); a "not done" result with no unmet line; (k) kills of the orchestrator inside a hand-back; (l) merged by a person while its worker is still running and the fake closeout answers "not done" (the hand-back waits for the worker); (m) a pull request that fully addresses its issue and says `Fixes`, so GitHub closes the issue at the merge. Until issue 668's pull request is merged the closeout is a fake; in the live test (PR E) the real skill is used | wave 8 for (a), (b), (c), (e) and (f), once through the skill call and once with the call made to fail twice, so that the fallback (K17) runs: when the skill cannot be called from inside a wave (U2, K17) or the call fails, wave 8 runs none of the skill's steps itself and never hands the closeout to a worker: it makes the second attempt in the same turn and, if that fails too, records the step `closeout_failed`, sets the issue's state file to `escalated` (so the issue leaves the places in flight, 5.7) and escalates as row 9 of 5.6 at once with the line "run `/sc:closeout <PR>` by hand"; wave 9 still releases the worker, a restart reads `closeout_failed` and tries once more, and a person runs the skill; (d) through waves 5 and 6 only | for (a) to (c), (e) and (f) wave 8 calls the closeout before the audit and `report-entry` (`--closed` for (a) to (c), `--update` for (e) and (f)), passes the pull request, the issue, the Dispatch and the entry key but no repo and no state file path, saves the result at `closeout_done` and passes it to `report-entry` as `--closeout-result`, so the entry shows it; (a) the entry is in `closed/` and the issue is closed; (b) the same, the orchestrator went to wave 8 without a second `gh pr merge`, the worker is not stopped and wave 9 releases it at its `worker_done`; (c) the call is made as for (a); (d) wave 8 is not run, nothing is closed or commented, and the case is escalated as row 9 of 5.6; a second run of wave 8 makes no second write by the orchestrator and a restart after `closeout_done` makes no second call; with the call made to fail twice, wave 8 records `closeout_failed` after its same-turn second attempt, the state file is `escalated` and the issue is not counted in flight, the row 9 escalation is raised at once with the line "run `/sc:closeout <PR>` by hand", wave 9 still releases the worker, and no `worker-start` and no terminal write is made for a closeout; `report-entry --closed` called by the skill and again by wave 8 gives the same final state in either order. (e) round 1: the pull request says `Refs <repo>#<number>` (ruling 38: it does not fully address the issue), so GitHub does not close the issue at the merge; the issue stays open with the short note of what remains, wave 8 runs `report-entry --update --round 1` and never `--closed` (the fake runner sees no `--closed` call in round 1), and the OPEN entry carries the line "merged, the issue is not finished: <the two unmet criteria> (round 1)" and the audit lines and is not moved; then `handback` prints `handback round=2`: the remaining file `.orchestrator/handbacks/<key>.r2.txt` holds `round: 2` and the two criteria, the state file is `in_progress` at wave 2 with `round: 2`, `remaining` and one item in `rounds`, the per-round ids are cleared, the old Dispatch is released (`worker-list` shows no reclaimable terminal for it), nothing is escalated, the poll's count shows the issue in flight in every cycle (the place is never freed) and a `--take` of the key refuses with `already_dispatched`; wave 2 calls the fake `/sc:issue --plan <N> --remaining <file>` once, `plan-check` passes and `body_sha` is recorded again; wave 4 reuses the worktree, makes the branch `issue-<N>-r2` from `origin/<default branch>`, fills the handoff `r2` file with the Round block and starts a new Task and a new Dispatch, never adopting the old worker; waves 5 to 7 run for the round-2 pull request, found by its new head branch; round 2's pull request fully addresses the remaining list and says `Fixes <repo>#<number>`, GitHub closes the issue at the merge and the fake closeout answers `already_closed`: the entry moves to `closed/` with the closeout and audit blocks of both rounds (`round=1` and `round=2`, none replaced), the issue is closed (by GitHub), wave 9 releases the second Dispatch, the state file ends `done` and the return contract prints `merged_closed` for the key; (f) wave 8 records `closeout_unclear`, runs `report-entry --update` (no `--closed`), wave 9 releases the worker's Dispatch, the state file is `escalated`, the entry stays open and is not `stalled` after the orchestrator has ended, the case is raised as row 9 in the issue's own entry with the one question for a person, the audit still runs because the pull request landed, and a later `--take` of the key renames the old file aside and creates a new one; (g) a `--take` of the seeded key refuses (the file is found by the exclusive create), nothing is moved (the file is where it was and `.orchestrator/issues/retaken/` has no file for the key), no worker is started, and with its orchestrator down the entry is `stalled`, not "landed, not finished"; (h) rounds 1 to 3 are each handed back, after round 4 the verb prints `stopped why=cap`, the state file is `escalated` with the last step `handback_stopped`, one row 9 escalation in the issue's own entry lists the round, the reason `cap` and the remaining items, no fifth worker is started, wave 9 releases the Dispatch of round 4, the entry is not `stalled` after the orchestrator has ended, and a later `--take` of the key renames the old file aside and starts at round 1; (i) round 1 is handed back, after round 2 the verb prints `stopped why=no_progress` at once (no third worker) with the same escalation and the reason `no_progress`; (j) the re-plan failing twice gives `handback --stop replan_failed`, a worktree with an edited tracked file gives `worktree_unusable` and no worker is started, while a worktree that holds only the copied claim folder `.dev/issues/<N>/` (untracked) is usable, the switch succeeds and the folder is still there, a "not done" result with no unmet line gives `stopped why=no_remaining`; each ends `escalated` with the last step `handback_stopped` and one row 9 escalation with its reason; (k) a kill after `handback_recorded` and before the re-plan, after `replan_written`, after `branch_switched` and after `old_dispatch_released`: after each restart the remaining file, the item of `rounds` and the plan marker of the round are written once, one Task and one Dispatch exist for the round, and no second hand-back happens; (l) wave 8 writes `handback_waiting`, the fake runner sees no `handback` call, no `worker-release`, no `git switch` and no second `worker-start`, wave 5's check for the still running worker keeps running and the entry shows the round line; a kill and restart while waiting repeats the check and still starts nothing; when the stand-in worker sends `worker_done`, wave 8 resumes at `handback_waiting` and runs the hand-back once (`handback round=2`, the release, the re-plan, the branch switch, one new Task and one new Dispatch); a worker that never settles is escalated under row 9 of 5.6 (the old rule 7) by the usual clock and no worker is started; the same pass is run with the stand-in reported as `unverifiable`, and then as `exited` with no `worker_done`: the fake runner sees no `handback`, no `worker-release`, no `git switch` and no `worker-start`, the state stays at `handback_waiting` and the row 9 escalation comes by the usual clock; (m) the fixture issue is closed by GitHub at the merge, the closeout returns `already_closed`, wave 8 runs `report-entry --closed` and the entry moves to `closed/`, nothing is handed back and the fake runner sees no reopen call anywhere (the skill never reopens). What the skill itself does in each case, including how it decides done, not done and unclear, is tested in issue 668 |
| T13 (added by this design, 5.11) | The audit on eight throwaway pull requests, and the merge-safety hold before a merge | 1 throwaway repo, a second account, and merged pull requests made to have: (a) nothing wrong; (b) a force push; (c) a thread resolved with no reply; (d) a change request by the second account at merge; (e) a committed conflict marker; (f) a deleted test file; (g) a changed CODEOWNERS; (h) a human comment `@codex fix this` followed by a commit by the review app; and, for the hold, open pull requests that (i) change a workflow file, (j) rename `.github/workflows/ci.yml` to a path outside the list, (k) have a diff over the limit of the files endpoint, and a read of the file list that fails (made to fail in the fake) | `superclaude orchestrator audit` on each, then wave 8; wave 6 and wave 7 on the open pull request that changes a workflow file | (a) clean; (b) AU1; (c) AU3; (d) AU4a and an escalation in the issue's own entry (and the gate of wave 6 would have refused this merge: `changes_requested`); (e) AU5 and an escalation in the issue's own entry; (f) AU6 as a warning only; (g) AU7 as a warning only (a merge made by a person, which the hold did not stop); (h) AU8 as a warning only, with the plain-English line and no agent named; (i) the workflow-file pull request is held by the gate with `merge_safety`, an escalation under row 8 appears in the issue's own entry with the question key `merge_safety:<PR>:<head sha>`, no `gh pr merge` runs while the pull request is green and reviewed, and it merges only after an OK recorded for that key through `answer-check`; a force push to a new head holds it again and the old OK does not pass; an OK recorded for another pull request, for an older head, or for a failing-check question on the same pull request and head does not clear it; (j) the rename out of `.github/workflows/` is held (its `previous_filename` matches); (k) with the diff over the limit, with the file list read failing and with a list shorter than `changed_files`, the gate prints `merge_safety` with the line "the file list of this pull request could not be read", no merge runs, and no case reads as "no match"; every result is in the closed entry; no GitHub write was made by the audit (the fake and the real runs show only GET and one query document) |

Before the PR is opened the author also records, for the live test of PR E: the real replies of S1 to S4, of `orchestration send --to run:<id>` from a plain shell (U5) and of `orca automations create` with the `*/5` trigger (U7).


## 10. Owner decisions

Fifteen choices change what the product does. The owner's rulings of 2026-11-18 and 2026-11-19 (the list the orchestrator keeps in `RULINGS.md`, cited here by its numbers and dates) decided eleven (OD1 to OD11), rulings 19 and 21 decided one (OD14), and rulings 23 to 35 (2026-11-19) decided the main choice of three more (OD12, OD13, OD15) and answered what was open under them; the few readings this design made itself are listed in each row. Rows of section 2 that point here carry the same OD number.

| OD | Question | Decision | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| OD1 | Workers push a branch and open a pull request; what do we allow? (ADD1, DQ7) | **Decided** (ruling 3, 2026-11-19 01:25 UTC): a worker pushes its own feature branch and opens its pull request through `/sc:pr-submit`; it never merges and never force pushes; only the orchestrator merges. The worker contract gets the "Orchestrated issue flow" section of ADD1. The worker runs with no launch-level guard, held by its written prompt and the orchestrator's merge rules (risk K12; `master` of IronClaude is not protected) | Decided |
| OD2 | What happens when something is assigned and no orchestrator of the repo is running (DQ2) | **Decided** (ruling 4, 2026-11-19): yes; the poll starts one, `ccsession orchestrator-<short>-<n>`, in the orchestrator repo's workspace | Decided |
| OD3 | Merge method per repo (DQ8) | **Decided** (ruling 5, 2026-11-19): the repo's normal practice, no question asked. The method lives in `repos.yaml`: `merge_method` for an ordinary pull request and `release_merge_method` for a release pull request (head `release_head`, base the default branch). IronClaude: `squash` and `merge` (integration to master); IronFlow and IronOrca: `merge`, UNVERIFIED as a settled practice (section 4.4) | Decided |
| OD4 | A pull request whose review agents (Augment, Codex) did not run or did not finish (ADD4) | **Decided** (ruling 6, 2026-11-19): the orchestrator holds the pull request and escalates to the human under rule 7 (a stall the orchestrator cannot unstick: no review agent shows activity on the final head, or one that started has not finished, 60 minutes after the head's checks finished; the 60 is this design's value, a constant of the skill); there is no merge-without-review option. A repo marked `reviewers: none` in `repos.yaml` has no review agent to wait for | Decided |
| OD5 | Hours of the worker status check (DQ10) | **Decided** (ruling 7, 2026-11-19): Monday to Friday, 24 hours a day, for each worker, ending for a worker the moment it sends `worker_done` (or wave 9 settles it); there is no working-hours setting | Decided |
| OD6 | One session or several? (DQ12, 5.7) | **Decided** (rulings 1 and 8, 2026-11-19): the report session `coordinator` stays one session and runs the daily report; issue work runs in one orchestrator per repo, at most 3 issues in flight each, with another orchestrator for the repo when it has more (section 5.7) | Decided |
| OD7 | When the poll is switched on, 17 open issues are already assigned to the user in IronbellyOrg (8 in IronClaude, 5 in IronOrca, 3 in Coder, 1 in Ironeye). Wake them all? (4.3) | **Decided** (ruling 9, 2026-11-19): record everything already assigned as seen at the first cycle and wake only later assignments; an older issue is handed over with `/sc:orchestrator --take <repo>#<N>` | Decided |
| OD8 | The name and org of the orchestrator repo | **Decided** (ruling 9 and the creation of 2026-11-19): `IronbellyOrg/IronOrchestrator`, private, holding `README.md`, `repos.yaml` and `overviews/` | Decided |
| OD9 | The built watcher promises "at most one launch per issue". The poll no longer launches the work. (7.1 row 2a) | **Decided** (ruling 9, 2026-11-19): accept "at most one claim and one worker start per issue (kept by the orchestrator), and up to 3 delivery attempts of a wake" | Decided |
| OD10 | Order: retire the old approval code first or after the new flow works? (8.2) | **Decided** (ruling 9, 2026-11-19): first, as a small pull request, no shim | Decided |
| OD11 | Which models plan, build and run the orchestrator? (5.8) | **Decided** (Clay, 2026-11-19 03:09 UTC): ccsession tiers, not model ids: `plan_tier` (default `tier1b`), `worker_tier` (default `tier2`) and `orchestrator_tier` (default `tier1b`) in `repos.yaml`, each overridable per repo; a tier the user cannot use falls once to the next allowed tier | Decided |
| OD12 | Does the poll hold an assigned issue until it passes the plan check, and who creates a missing plan? (4.11, A) | **Decided by rulings 23 to 35 (rulings 23, 30, 31, 33):** yes. The POLL labels an assigned issue that lacks the three sections `hold:plan` and posts no comment (ruling 33); it does not send the issue to an orchestrator while held; once lifted it reaches the orchestrator on the next cycle; while held the system watches for plan work elsewhere and waits; after 1 hour with none the poll sends one `plan_request` to the repo's orchestrator, which starts the plan agent (ruling 30), and all plan creation goes through a new mode `/sc:issue --plan` built as PR P before PR B (ruling 31); a hold removed by hand is removed and not put back; an edit after the claim by someone other than the claimer is one line in the report entry; `hold:backlog` means the system does nothing at all. **Settled as recommended:** AD-A9 (a hold removed by hand while the plan is missing; a reading this design made; ruling 39) | Decided |
| OD13 | What may reach Clay, how often, and what happens when he does not answer? (5.6, B) | **Decided by rulings 23 to 29 (ruling 24):** ruling 16 fixes his five product reasons and ruling 17 the plain-English form; one table of nine rows; the rules are on from the start (the ladder and `reach_bar` were removed by ruling 24); an unanswered question waits, no default; tagging and publishing a release need a recorded human OK or directive; notices and escalations appear inside the issue's own entry in the living report; no taps or clicks. **Settled as recommended:** AD-B4 (the daily cap of 5 a day per repo; ruling 39) | Decided |
| OD14 | Is closeout its own skill, and how does 655 use it? (5.10, D) | **Decided** by rulings 19 and 21 (2026-11-19): closeout is its own issue and skill, #668, with its own spec, reviews and approval by Clay, and it adopts Jay's skill (ruling 22). Wave 8 of 655 calls `/sc:closeout`, and issue 668 is a dependency that must be merged before the live test. **Decided by ruling 26:** `/sc:closeout` closes the issue (wave 8 directs the skill to it) and every worker pull request links its issue, with `Fixes <repo>#<number>` only when it fully addresses the issue and `Refs <repo>#<number>` otherwise (ruling 38). **Decided by ruling 32 (issue 668's D18):** the closeout does not decide by the words `Fixes`, `Closes` or `Refs`; it decides whether the issue is done by reading the issue's own done-criteria against what the pull request and its linked pull requests delivered, closes a done issue with a note saying why, leaves a not-done issue open with a short note of what remains, and escalates only when it still cannot tell. **Decided by ruling 36 (2026-11-19 20:59 UTC), AD-D4:** a not-done issue goes back to the orchestrator, which records what remains, updates the plan through `/sc:issue --plan` and starts a new worker through the same waves; it reaches Clay only under the table of 5.6 (bound and mechanism: AD-D5 to AD-D7, settled by default unless Clay objects). A, B, C and E are verbs, not skills | Decided |
| OD15 | Which audit checks run after a pull request lands, and which reach Clay? (5.11, E) | **Decided by rulings 23 to 35 (rulings 27, 34, 35):** the eight checks of Jay's audit adapted as AU1 to AU8; violations AU4a, AU4c and AU5 reach him, everything else stays in the entry; a pull request with any open "changes requested" review is never merged (the gate reason `changes_requested` is a hard refusal); a bot-fix commit after a "@codex fix this" style request is flagged as a warning (AD-E2); a pull request that changes CI or another merge-safety file waits for Clay's explicit OK before the merge, a hard hold that is escalated (AD-E4, 5.3 wave 7). **Settled as recommended:** AD-E5 (the default list of merge-safety files; ruling 39) | Decided |

C has no OD row: the "may never" list is Clay's own request and rulings 17, 24, 25 and 29 settle what is in it (the orchestrator may interrupt a worker for one case, it may delete the ordinary files its work needs, and the restart of an ended worker stays); its two earlier conflicts with the existing design (AD-C1, and the removal of "deleting ordinary files" from 5.6) are settled by rulings 25 and 29. The exceptions that the interrupt needs are in 6.3: FR-10's acceptance line (C25), the out-of-scope list (C26), the 60-second boundary of FR-23 and wave 5 rule (2) of this design (C27), the acceptance box of that boundary at line 263 (C29) and the FR-10 proof row (C28): five places of the coordinator spec and one of this design.

**Choices under the owner decisions (AD ids).** The AD ids name the smaller choices that sit under the owner decisions above; each row names the OD it belongs to. Nothing is open: 21 were decided by Clay's rulings 23 to 35 (13:16, 13:21 and 13:34 UTC), AD-A9, AD-B4 and AD-E5 were settled as recommended (ruling 39) and AD-D4 by ruling 36, and AD-D5 to AD-D7 are settled by default (the recommendation of the orchestrator session, which stands unless the owner objects).

Readings settled as recommended or by ruling 36:

| ID | The question, in plain English | What the words mean | Example | Reading, and how it was settled | OD and part |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AD-A9 | What happens to an issue whose hold a person removed by hand while the plan is still missing? | Ruling 23: a hold removed by hand is removed and not put back; if an issue later arrives with no plan, the same flow applies. | Clay takes `hold:plan` off issue 12 while its Test plan is empty. Reading 1: the poll leaves it alone (no label, no wake) until it passes the check or is assigned again. Reading 2: the poll wakes it and the orchestrator's check at the start of wave 1 refuses it | Reading 1: `hold_removed_by_hand`, nothing is written, nothing is woken, and an issue that leaves the assigned list and returns arrives anew and is held afresh. The poll never puts the label back and never starts work that has no plan. **Settled as recommended** (ruling 39) | OD12, A |
| AD-B4 | How many notices a day, and are entries ever dropped? (not ruled on; open, and no longer tied to a ladder) | A notice is a ClickUp DM or an unread mark. An entry is the escalation in the issue's own entry on the page. A cap holds notices back; it never removes an entry. | With a cap of 5 a sixth DM in 24 hours waits for the next hourly pass with room; its entry is already on the page | Cap of 5 per repo per 24 hours, notices only, entries never dropped (FR-10 is "never loss"). 5 is this design's number; Jay's own default is 0, no cap. **Settled as recommended** (ruling 39) | OD13, B |
| AD-D4 | What does wave 8 do with an issue that the closeout finds not done? (new: ruling 32 says the issue stays open with a short note of what remains, and is not an escalation, but not what the orchestrator does with its state, its place in flight and its entry) | After a merge the closeout decides whether the issue is done. "Not done" means the pull request landed but part of the issue's own criteria is still open. The orchestrator's state file, its place in flight (at most 3 per orchestrator) and the report entry still have to end somewhere. | A pull request lands the first of three stages of a multi-part issue. Option 1 (this row's first reading, now dropped): the orchestrator ends its state file with a new status, frees the place, leaves the entry open and lets a person take the issue again by hand. Option 2: the orchestrator keeps the issue in flight and starts the next stage by itself | **Settled by Clay's ruling 36 (2026-11-19 20:59 UTC), option 2:** "If closeout runs and finds the PR didn't finish the issue, it should escalate back to the orchestrator to initiate the work that was not done, following the same workflow." The not-done verdict goes back to the orchestrator and not to Clay: it records what remains, updates the plan through `/sc:issue --plan`, and starts a new worker through the same waves; it reaches Clay only under the table of 5.6. The mechanism is in 5.3 wave 8 (the hand-back) and the three details AD-D5 to AD-D7. No status is added to the set of 5.5 and no outcome to the set of 5.2 | OD14, D |
| AD-E5 | Which files are on the default merge-safety list? (new: ruling 35 says "CI or another merge-safety file" and asks for the list to be a field per repo; the default is the 655 side's recommendation to confirm) | `merge_safety_files` is a list of glob patterns per repo in `repos.yaml`. A pull request that changes a file it matches waits for Clay's explicit OK before the merge. | A pull request edits `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, or `.claude/settings.json` of IronClaude | `.github/workflows/**`; the branch protection and ruleset files if any (`.github/rulesets/**`, `.github/settings.yml`); `CODEOWNERS`, `.github/CODEOWNERS`, `docs/CODEOWNERS`; and the files the repo's own merge gate reads (IronClaude: `.claude/settings.json`, `src/superclaude/hooks/**`, `plugins/superclaude/hooks/**`; the other repos list theirs when `repos.yaml` is filled in). **Settled as recommended** (ruling 39) | OD15, E |

Decided by Clay's rulings (2026-11-19, 13:16, 13:21 and 13:34 UTC), with his words where the ruling has them; issue 668's D18 is followed by 5.10:

| ID | Decided | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| AD-A1 | Who writes a missing plan: an agent is started after one hour with no plan and no sign of plan work, and all plan creation goes through `/sc:issue`; how it starts and how it goes through `/sc:issue` are AD-A8 and AD-A7 below | 23 |
| AD-A2 | A hold removed by hand is removed and not put back (differs from Jay's guard on purpose); what happens next is AD-A9 | 23 |
| AD-A3 | Before the claim edits are fine; after the claim an edit by someone other than the claimer is one line in the report entry and nothing else; the live plan check at the start of wave 1 stays | 23 |
| AD-A4 | The label is `hold:plan`; a second label `hold:backlog` means the system does nothing at all with the issue until it is removed | 23 |
| AD-A5 | One hour, not 30 minutes | 23 |
| AD-A6 | Hold label only, no comment (as Jay does): the poll posts nothing on a held issue. Clay: "hold label only, no comment (as Jay does)" | 33 |
| AD-A7 | All plan creation flows through `/sc:issue`: a mode that fills the three sections of an existing issue (`/sc:issue --plan <N>`), reusing create's drafting workers and the #651 templates, as its own small pull request (PR P) built before PR B; 8.3 names the files and the smallest mechanism. Clay: "all plan creation flows through /sc:issue" | 31 |
| AD-A8 | After 1 hour the poll sends ONE `plan_request` wake item to the repo's orchestrator; the issue stays held and unclaimed; the orchestrator starts the plan agent; the poll lifts the hold when the plan exists. Clay: "the orchestrator needs to kick off an agent to investigate and create the plan" | 30 |
| AD-B1 | An unanswered question waits; no default | 24 |
| AD-B2 | The reach-Clay rules are on from the start; the ladder and the key `reach_bar` are removed | 24 |
| AD-B3 | Merging a release pull request is merging (ruling 13); tagging and publishing a release always need a human OK or directive; the orchestrator may do them only with it | 24 |
| AD-B5 | No taps or clicks; an answer is typed | 24 |
| AD-C1 | The orchestrator may interrupt a worker when a major issue must be escalated to Clay (reverses the earlier "no") | 25 |
| AD-D2 | `/sc:closeout` closes the issue; wave 8 directs the skill to it | 26 |
| AD-D3 | Every worker pull request links its issue (`Fixes <repo>#<number>` only when it fully addresses the issue, otherwise `Refs <repo>#<number>`): ruling 26, clarified by ruling 38 | 26, 38 |
| AD-E1 | Only AU4a, AU4c and AU5 reach Clay, as recommended | 27 |
| AD-E2 | Yes: flag a "@codex fix this" style bot fix (a commit by the review app after such a request) as a warning in the audit; it never blocks a merge | 34 |
| AD-E3 | A pull request with any open "changes requested" review is never merged; the gate reason `changes_requested` is a hard refusal | 27 |
| AD-E4 | A pull request that changes CI or another merge-safety file waits for Clay's explicit OK before the merge (a hard hold, escalated), even when green and reviewed; the file list is a field of `repos.yaml` (its default is AD-E5) | 35 |
| issue 668 D18 | Any pull request that fully addresses an issue closes that issue. `/sc:closeout` does not decide by the words `Fixes`, `Closes` or `Refs`; it determines whether the issue is done by reading the issue's own criteria against what the pull request and its linked pull requests delivered; fully addressed: close it with a note saying why; not done: leave it open with a short note of what remains; unclear: investigate and decide, and escalate only if it still cannot tell. A model reads and decides; the scripts keep the mechanical parts | 32 |
| AD-X1 | As recommended: the handoff line is the rule, the orchestrator's own writers are scanned | 28 |
| AD-X2 | As recommended: tier names stay in the two entry lines, never the model string | 28 |

Settled by default (ruling 36 leaves these details; the recommendation of the orchestrator session, which stands unless the owner objects):

| ID | Settled by default | Why | OD and part |
|---|---|---|---|
| AD-D5 | At most 3 hand-backs per issue (4 rounds, constant `HANDBACK_MAX`), and an immediate stop when a round leaves the same unmet criteria as the round before (no progress). Either stop is row 9 of 5.6 ("stuck, nobody else can unstick it") in the issue's own entry, listing the round, the reason and the remaining items; the state file is `escalated` with the last step `handback_stopped` | Ruling 36 says "escalates to Clay only under the normal rules": a loop with no bound would be an unbounded cost and a stuck issue nobody sees. 3 is this design's value, not measured; the cap is one constant | OD14, D |
| AD-D6 | The issue re-enters the waves at wave 2 with the state file `in_progress`, `round` n+1 and `remaining` set, so the place stays counted; the old worker's Dispatch is released; the new worker starts in the reused worktree on a new branch `issue-<N>-r<n>` made from `origin/<default branch>` when no tracked file is changed there (untracked files, such as the copied claim folder, do not count); waves 1 and 3 are not run again; and none of this starts until the old worker's Dispatch is final. The deterministic part is the verb `handback` | "Following the same workflow" means the same waves from the handoff: a new Task and Dispatch, a new pull request, the same gate, merge, closeout and audit. A new worktree is not needed: the script that makes the worktree reuses `issue-<N>` | OD14, D |
| AD-D7 | The plan is updated by one more option of PR P's mode, `/sc:issue --plan <N> --remaining <file>`, which replaces the Fix approach and Test plan sections with the remaining scope, keeps the done items listed as done, and makes one marked body write. It is part of PR P, not a new pull request, and not a free-form write | Clay's ruling 31 and 36: plan work goes through `/sc:issue --plan`. Plain plan mode stops when a plan exists, and a left-open issue has one, so it needs this option | OD14, D |


## 11. Risks

Issue 655 lists eleven risks (K1 to K11); each keeps its mitigation as written there, and this design adds the rows marked new. Likelihood and impact are this design's estimate.

| # | Risk | L / I | Mitigation | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K1 | Assignment by anyone with triage or write access starts a worker | M / H | Intended gate (ruling R2); the repo list is an allow list; the day cap of 6; one claim per issue; no GitHub text reaches the poll's typed line | DQ3, OQ6, 3.2 |
| K2 | Issue text reaches the worker through the handoff prompt | M / H | The template quotes it in a data fence, never as instruction | 5.4 |
| K3 | Multi-repo work widens the origin check | M / H | The repo list comes from the orchestrator repo; origin check per repo; every query pinned to a listed org; every row re-checked against the list | 4.4 |
| K4 | The claim's launch levels and `worker-start` could be mixed | L / H | `worker-start` only; a test fails if the skill launches through `orca_worktree.py launch` | DQ7, 9.1 |
| K5 | This design and the IronFlow coordinator spec drift apart | M / M | One design, FRs cited by number, the spec moved and changed per 6.3, `diff -q` of the two copies | 6.1 |
| K6 | The skill is one large pull request | H / M | The local end-to-end tests (T1 to T13) before the PR; the regression gate; the deterministic parts are `superclaude orchestrator` verbs with their own tests | 8.1, 9.2 |
| K7 | The orchestrator can merge and close | M / H | The merge conditions as a gate verb; `--match-head-commit`; no `--auto`, `--admin` or `--delete-branch`; no merge over a failing check without a recorded OK; every `gh` call pinned; the standing grant list | DQ8, ADD5, 5.6 |
| K8 | The orchestrator repo does not exist yet | H / M | Section 6.2 defines its minimum format and who keeps it | 6.2 |
| K9 | Removing the approval gate removes code guard G2 relies on | L / H | Each G2 rule is listed; rule 1 goes, rule 2 stays; an independent review of the guard change | 7.3 |
| K10 | Unknown GitHub error text | M / M | Treated as a login failure until the live test records it | 4.6 |
| K11 | The wake mechanism is unproven here | M / M | Copied from a pattern that runs (the report waker); two channels and an acknowledgment; the live test covers it | 3, U3, U5 |
| K12 (new) | Workers run with no launch-level guard and no L2 pre-push hook, and with push rights on their own branch; IronClaude `master` is not protected, so only the worker's written prompt (never merge, never force-push) holds it back from pushing there | M / H | The handoff prompt and the contract section of ADD1; the orchestrator, not the worker, merges; the live test proves the worker is unbound and records what it can reach | DQ7, OD1 |
| K13 (new) | Two orchestrators of one user both claim (the claim is idempotent for the same login) | M / M | The lease comment with lowest-id arbitration | DQ3 |
| K14 (new) | A wake is lost while the session is busy for a long turn | M / M | The mailbox channel and the acknowledgment with 3 attempts; an unread mark after the third; OD6 | DQ2, OD6 |
| K15 (new) | Switch-on wakes 17 issues already assigned | H / M | Baseline at the first cycle (OD7) and the day cap | 4.3 |
| K16 (new) | The `wake.py` port duplicates about 100 lines of the report skill's `orca_term.py` | M / L | Accepted for now; after PR C both live in IronClaude and a follow-up makes the report waker call the package module | 4.10 |
| K17 (new) | The orchestrator session starts a Skill inside a Skill (`/sc:issue` from `/sc:orchestrator`) and the claim assumes a repo directory | M / M | The first local end-to-end test checks both; the fallback is a claim run by a worker in the checkout | ADD2, U1, U2 |
| K18 (new) | The search API shows an assignment late or incompletely | M / L | The next poll finds it; `incomplete_results` is treated as a failed read; the live test measures the delay | ADD3, U8 |
| K19 (new) | An Orca upgrade changes the verbs the skill names | M / M | A fixture of `orca orchestration --help` pins the verbs and a test fails on a name that is not in it; the Orca version is written into each return contract | 9.1 |
| K20 (new) | The merge happens, then the issue-closing keyword in the pull request already closed the issue, or the reverse | L / L | The closeout skill (issue 668) reads the issue state first and closes only if the issue is open and done (ruling 32) | 5.3 |
| K21 (new) | Several orchestrator sessions: a count that is wrong lets a repo exceed 3 issues in flight, or a dead session leaves issues unattended | M / M | The count is read from the state files and the poll's own entries and a damaged file counts as in flight; the dead-orchestrator restart with the same tag and Run; the 30-minute idle end; the day cap of 6 new issues a day bounds new orchestrator ids to 6 a day, and the 3-issue limit and the idle end keep the live sessions few | 5.7 |
| K22 (new) | The "may never" list binds the verbs, not a command the model types itself | M / M | `refuse` in every verb; a text scan of the skill; the stated limit; U42 asks whether a hook can extend it | 5.9 |
| K23 (new) | The hold check is mechanical, so a plan the agent drafts can pass it with thin text | M / M | The hold guards against empty issues, not poor plans; the worker brief and the reviewers judge the plan; stated in 4.11 point 10 | 4.11 |
| K24 (new) | A hold, the one-hour quiet period and the poll cycles (one to lift the hold, one to wake the issue) delay an unfilled issue by up to an hour plus about 10 minutes, and the plan call on top | H / L | Intended (ruling 23: 1 hour); an issue that passes starts at once; T11 measures it | 4.11 |
| K25 (new) | The unanswered rule stops an issue for as long as Clay does not answer | M / M | Only that issue waits; the cap holds back notices, never entries; the entry says what stays blocked | 5.6 |
| K26 (new) | A linked branch, pull request or session counts as plan work, so an old session that stays open keeps its issue held, and a hold removed by hand leaves an issue that is neither held nor woken | M / L | The `status` output names the signal that holds each issue; a person who wants it started fills in the three sections or assigns it again (AD-A9); the signals older than an hour do not count | 4.11 points 5 and 10 |
| K27 (new) | The plan call is a Skill call of `/sc:issue --plan` made by the orchestrator session from inside wave 0, because `/sc:issue` is on the never-as-worker list (a worker cannot run it): K17's question again, for a second call | M / M | PR P is merged before PR B opens; PR B's tests use a fake of the call; T11 case (c) is the live check; if the call fails twice the issue stays held and the failure is escalated (4.11 point 5) | 4.11 point 5, 8.3 |
| K28 (new, ruling 36) | The hand-back loop costs a worker, a plan update and a merge for each round, and a wrong "not done" verdict can send an issue round again or end it early; the hole that a part-done pull request saying `Fixes` makes GitHub close the issue at the merge, so the closeout sees `already_closed` and no hand-back happens, is answered by ruling 38 (the worker writes `Fixes` only when its pull request fully addresses the issue, otherwise `Refs`); the residual, stated as a limit: a worker that writes `Fixes` wrongly still closes the issue at the merge and the closeout leaves it alone (it never reopens), a worker's judgment and not a script check (U60 (4)) | M / M | At most 3 hand-backs per issue (4 rounds) and an immediate stop when a round leaves the same unmet criteria (AD-D5), both in the verb `handback`; every stop is a row 9 escalation in the issue's own entry with the remaining items; every round is a normal pull request that the merge gate, the reviews and the audit judge; the link word of the handoff (ruling 38, a test on the template); T12 (e), (h) to (m) | 5.3 wave 8 |
